BALASEVICIUS – Tony Balasevicius is a retired military officer. During
his career he has served with the Canadian Airborne Regiment, and Joint
Task Force Two (JTF 2). He has worked in the Department of Applied
Military Science at the Royal Military College of Canada (RMC) and as
an Analyst with the Directorate Future Security Analysis (Chief Force
Development), where his main research focus was on the future Arctic
security environment. He was Team Leader of the Concepts Group at the
Canadian Forces Warfare Centre (CFWC) and has worked for the Chief
Defence Intelligence (Directorate of Science and technical Intelligence)
and with the Canadian Forces Land Warfare Centre. He has a Master’s
degree in War Studies from RMC. He has completed the Land Force
Technical Staff Programme and Canadian Forces Staff College. He is
currently doing contract work in the Defence and Security field. His
research interests include China and Russia’s military modernization and
their development of Hybrid Warfare, the future of SOF and the growing
influence of non-state actors on the battlefield.

BOUFFARD – Dr. Troy J. Bouffard is the Director of the Center for
Arctic Security and Resilience, University of Alaska Fairbanks. His
research interests include Arctic Security, Russian military in the Arctic,
and resiliency in the Circumpolar North.

Chieves, Belgium. He has authored numerous books and articles and was
appointed as the Honorary Colonel of the Canadian Grenadier Guards.

THOMPSON – Ms. Christiane Thompson is an assistant professor of inter-
disciplinary studies at JSOU with close to 30 years of experience working
for the U.S. Department of Defense. She holds an MA in political science
from the University of Akron; she completed advanced military educa-
tion at the U.S. Army Command and General Staff College and received an
MMAS from the U.S. Army School of Advanced Military Studies; and she
holds an ALM degree in Sustainability from Harvard University, Harvard
Extension School.

                                                                           BALASEVICIUS
                                                                           COOMBS &
                                                                           HORN,
                                                                   T H E A R C T I C : I m p l i ca t i o n s fo r S O F
                                                                                                                    THE NORTHERN FLANK
                                                                                                                                         THE
The Arctic, distant, isolated and with a harsh environment
has for decades remained a region of cooperation and arguably
indifference. However, the relentless effects of climate change
                                                                                                                                         NORTHERN
                                                                                                                                         FLANK
and the profound shifts in the global geo-political landscape
have made the Arctic a rapidly evolving arena of competition
and insecurity. In this dynamic reality, security adaptation and
resilience are paramount, with significant implications for SOF.



                                                                                                                                         THE ARCTIC:
                                                                                                                                         IMPLICATIONS FOR SOF
                                                                                                                                         BERND HORN, HOWARD G. COOMBS & TONY BALASEVICIUS
                                                                                                                                         EDITORS
